Records carry only facts (validator rejects descriptions/images); source shown is the official site. Workflow validates the dataset, upserts L2 records into D1 via scripts/import-servers.mjs --remote --games lineage2 --yes (idempotent; L2 online-source configs already live in the dataset, so no source is disabled), commits import/lineage2.json to main with GITHUB_TOKEN (no deploy loop). # GameScout — текущее состояние и журнал передачи работы




**Обновлено: 07.10.2026. Ядро НЕ готово к приемке.** Этот файл — первая точка входа для любого ИИ или разработчика. Сначала читать **«Состояние сейчас»**, **«Следующие действия»**, **«Блокеры»** и только затем исторический журнал ниже. Объем и критерии приемки: [roadmap](roadmap-2026-09-27.md); архитектура и исходный аудит: [Reality Check](core-reality-check.md). Старые записи ниже описывают состояние *на дату их написания* и не отменяют актуальную сводку.

## Правило ведения файла — обязательно для каждого следующего исполнителя

1. Перед изменениями прочитать этот файл, roadmap, релевантные runbook/миграции и `git status`; сверить важные факты с текущими API/окружением. Не повторять работу, уже подтвержденную ниже, без причины. Работать на текущей ветке и учитывать параллельную работу над визуалом.
2. **После каждого значимого шага** сразу обновить сводку и дописать запись в журнал: изменение кода/конфигурации, деплой, миграцию, проверку, обнаруженный блокер, откат или смену плана. Не ждать конца сессии. При неудаче указать число попыток и результат; после трех безуспешных попыток переключиться на независимую задачу.
3. Каждая запись должна отвечать: **что сделано; где** (файлы, commit/PR, сервис и окружение); **что реально работает**; **чем проверено** (команда/HTTP/браузер и результат); **что осталось/заблокировано**; **стоимость/новый ресурс**; **откат** для измененного состояния; **следующий шаг**. Отделять локальное, staging и production. Не считать сборку фронтенда доказательством сквозного сценария.
4. Использовать метки **VERIFIED** (наблюдалось), **LIKELY** (сильные косвенные данные), **HYPOTHESIS** (нужно проверить), **BLOCKED** (конкретная причина и действие для снятия). «Почти сделано» означает код/настройки готовы, но указанный end-to-end тест не пройден. Не записывать предположение как завершенную функцию.
5. Не помещать в Markdown секреты, токены, персональные данные, содержимое писем или ключи. Указывать лишь безопасные имена переменных, место хранения и владельца следующего действия. После каждого завершенного среза делать отдельный commit и обновлять этот файл в том же срезе. Если внешнее действие выполнено до commit, зафиксировать его немедленно отдельной записью.
6. Не переключать production DNS, реальные платежи и необратимые миграции без отдельного согласования результата и отката. Если автоматическая проверка разрешений отклонила действие, не обходить ее другим способом: записать отказ, продолжить независимую работу и запросить точное разрешение.

**03.10 — скины миров (Claude), source + local VERIFIED:** три мира получили собственный вид без новых картинок. Lineage 2: гравированные заглавные Cormorant SC, золотые уголки на панелях; Minecraft: Pixelify Sans, тёмное дерево с фаской как у блока; GTA RP: Oswald курсивом, неоновые рамки. Шрифты самохостятся через @fontsource (OFL, латиница и кириллица, без Google Fonts), подключены в src/theme/worldFonts.ts. Оформление панелей и строк задаётся в index.css по data-pattern (stone, voxel, neon), поэтому подходит и для хроник. **02.10 — первый GitHub production deploy BLOCKED до записи:** run `36962896416` успешно прошёл private56/56, live catalog70/70, SEO/reconciliation0 и bundle gate; оба GitHub secrets присутствуют. Шаги upload/production verification не запускались, поэтому production этим run не изменён и rollback не требовался. VERIFIED причина на границе IAM: ключи распознаны, но сервисному аккаунту не хватает доступа к bucket либо роль не назначена на этот bucket. По документации Yandex роль `storage.uploader` включает list/read/upload и подходит текущему workflow без delete. Workflow обновлён: теперь read-access preflight выполняется до долгих тестов и выдаёт понятную ошибку. Следом владелец один раз назначает `storage.uploader` сервисному аккаунту на Object Storage → `gamescout-ru-site`, затем повторный ручной deploy.

Central egress пока покрывает raw AI запрет и Telegram delivery; transactional/public query policies еще не закрыты. Sonnet и independent outage probe пока не реализованы. Ниже промежуточные записи описывают ход этой сессии, а не отменяют этот результат.

## Следующие действия по приоритету

1. **P0 — проверка контролируемого Claim.** Код SITE/DNS/MANUAL и ADMIN-only grant опубликованы. Пройти действительный Claim и изменение своей карточки→outbox→D1→сайт. Сейчас owner grants=0; чужие реальные серверы не использовать для фиктивных заявок. Minecraft MOTD/FiveM auto-check отложен до проверяемого адреса в registry; ручной fallback доступен.
2. **P1 — Robokassa и возвраты.** Без credentials можно завершать учет происхождения купленных Coins, израсходованных услуг и допустимой неиспользованной части, full/partial refund invariants. Существующий возврат Поддержки не означает возврат рублей провайдером. Владелец 01.10 сообщил, что кабинет не создавал; живой TEST/чеки BLOCKED. Продажи выключены.
3. **P1 — реальная защита и QA.** Проверить trusted IP/ASN/Turnstile и доступный бесплатный периметр Yandex gateway; Emergency развернут выключенным. Пройти роли/3 игры/мобильные авторизованные состояния на контролируемых данных. Локальные56 тестов и layout/SEO проверки не заменяют production E2E.
4. **P2 — завершение выпуска.** Автоматизировать обновление статического HTML после каталожных изменений; проверить юридические/почтовые условия перед регистрацией. Метрика отложена владельцем; вебмастера при доступе. SHOULD HAVE/LATER не расширять автоматически. Projection70/70, Telegram relay и cold backup уже закрыты — повторно не настраивать без нового дефекта.

Нулевой баланс не подставляется вместо ошибки, запоздалый запрос другого аккаунта не применяется. **Проверено:** TypeScript; внутренний браузер показал существующий ADMIN с 0 Coins/пустой историей, после выхода и входа синтетического local игрока — 10 тестовых Coins и корректировку +10; обновление работает на ширине 390. Малый overflow всей страницы обнаружен, длинный email получил перенос; новая карточка помещается. Live это не проверено. Локальные dev-сервисы запущены на 8789/5173, local миграция 21 применена стандартным `--local`; production не менялся. Стоимость: 0 ₽ новых ресурсов. Откат — убрать компонент/импорт. Следом: отдельный commit и развертывание после preflight.

Не повторять через другой канал до явного разрешения. Worker secret можно при необходимости удалить или заменить; изменение публичных карточек от этого шага не произошло.

## Исторический журнал срезов

Записи ниже сохранены для проверки происхождения решений. Их фразы «не развернуто», «нет бэкапа», «D1 не изменена» могут быть устаревшими; актуальное состояние указано выше.

## Slice 1: private identity, Claim, public outbox, People's rating (local)

**Implemented.** `private-api/migrations/001_identity.sql` and `002_votes.sql` define PostgreSQL-owned identities, session hashes, one-time tokens, OAuth states, server grants, claims, audit, mail/public outboxes, and rolling votes. `private-api/app.mjs` implements email-link registration/verification/reset, password login, session revocation, subject-based Google/VK/Yandex/Discord OAuth with explicit account linking, Claim review, per-server permissions, and verified-email votes. Rate limits and account/device/IP signals gate voting; ASN is recorded for later review. High-risk votes require server-verified Turnstile. Scrypt hashes passwords; private mail links are encrypted at rest in the outbox. Do not claim these signals identify a unique human.

The private transaction writes the authoritative mutation and an outbox event together. `private-api/outbox.mjs` retries delivery; `worker/lib/public-projection.ts` accepts only public server fields and a monotonic per-server version. The D1 migration `0007_public_projection.sql` is additive and **local only**. `PUBLIC_ONLY` can close legacy private Worker routes at cutover. No cross-database atomic write is needed. Delivery lag is queryable; an operational alert is still needed.

Local UI at `npm run dev:api`, `npm run dev:private`, and `npm run dev:core-ui` exposes `/account`, Claim, and voting. The local mail link is visible at `http://127.0.0.1:8789/__dev/mail`; this endpoint exists only with `--local`. Production has no implicit database migration or mail fallback. The old D1 account route no longer merges OAuth users just because emails match; VK state is checked.

Catalog and server page edits remove VIP/TOP visual treatment and show **«Онлайн не указан»** for unknown online. Empty online peaks/averages are shown as dashes. `/rating` in private mode reads votes from PostgreSQL, with separate L2/Minecraft/GTA filters and no paid ordering. The old D1 votes sort is hidden in private mode because it is not the new rolling rating. Demo pages stay `noindex`; generated demo media is explicitly marked as such. Full unified server-page information and Support are still outstanding.

**Verified.** Six PostgreSQL/PGlite invariant tests pass for one-time credentials, expiry/races, non-merging OAuth, Claim player→owner→admin across L2/Minecraft/GTA, outbox retry/stale/PII/rollback, and vote window/idempotency/risk. Legacy account acceptance passed (output report), all 35 monitoring adapter tests passed, TypeScript check and Vite build passed. In-app browser at `127.0.0.1:5173` showed three GTA RP demo cards, server page with honest unknown metrics, `/rating` with an empty rolling window, and sign-in link to `/account`. This is local evidence, not production acceptance. The build warns about a large JS chunk.

**Rollback.** No production state to roll back. To return local UI to legacy mode, run normal `npm run dev` instead of `dev:core-ui`, stop private API, and leave `PUBLIC_ONLY` unset. D1 migration 0007 is additive; do not roll back a live projection by dropping it without first quiescing delivery and checking its queue. PostgreSQL migrations are intended for a new, empty database and need a backup/restore plan before a live deployment. Existing D1 private records require an explicit, verified export/import and temporary write freeze before cutover; deleting them is out of scope.

**BLOCKED for live acceptance.** No Yandex PostgreSQL cluster or deployed private API topology; no verified email sender, production OAuth app secrets/callbacks, Turnstile credentials, or production signing/encryption secrets. Existing user data still lives in production D1 until an approved migration. Live browser OAuth and mail delivery cannot be claimed. The local test uses generated keys and PGlite, never production credentials.

**Cost.** 0 ₽ in newly created cloud resources. Yandex price estimate and assumptions are in the Reality Check; approve the exact region, HA topology, disk, backup, and monthly budget before creating resources.

## Slice 2: factual catalogs and responsive server pages (local)

`ServerRow` now exposes the same version, rates/mode, opening date and region facts for L2, Minecraft and GTA RP at phone and desktop widths, with a visible «Подробнее» action. Missing values say so. Current online appears only for a fresh known measurement, with its source and relative update time; missing measurement hides the capacity bar and trend. The server page no longer labels an unknown count as current, shows an unknown position when no rolling votes exist, and re-reads the ranking after a vote instead of incrementing the UI on an idempotent replay. Demo `example.com` links and their «Играть» CTA are suppressed.

**Verified:** TypeScript check and an in-app browser at 390 px for all three game catalogs and the L2/Minecraft profiles. Missing online and peaks stayed unknown; the mobile Minecraft headline no longer overflowed. The browser viewport was restored. This does not verify non-demo pages against a live measurement. Support and its position/statistics block remain part of slice 4; stage 2 is not fully accepted until those data are present.

**Rollback/cost:** presentation-only changes can be reverted at this commit; private schema and production state were not touched. 0 ₽ additional cloud resources. Next: complete organic ordering and risk challenges, then append-only Coins and Support.

## Slice 3: organic catalog order and vote risk challenge (local)

The catalog's «Народный рейтинг» order now reads rolling accepted votes from the private PostgreSQL API and hydrates only public server cards from D1. Equal or zero-vote cards get a stable name/id tie-breaker; the old D1 votes counter, paid promotion and ownership are absent from this formula. Current implementation reads all matching public cards client-side, so it needs a bounded server-side join or public aggregate projection before a large-scale release. API pagination prevents a fixed top-100 cutoff.

Migration `003_vote_risk.sql` adds an adjustable ASN threshold; device/IP/ASN counts, missing device signal and implausibly fast page interaction trigger a challenge. The browser can render Turnstile explicitly when configured, then send its token to the private API. The server validates the token, hostname and `vote` action using Siteverify; it never treats a browser claim as proof. [Cloudflare's official client](https://developers.cloudflare.com/turnstile/get-started/client-side-rendering/) and [server validation](https://developers.cloudflare.com/turnstile/get-started/server-side-validation/) documentation were checked. IP/ASN must be supplied by a trusted ingress; the current local listener knows the socket IP but has no trustworthy ASN feed.

**Verified:** seven PGlite tests cover voting in all three games, pagination, missing/fast signals, an ASN threshold, invalid and valid challenge responses; TypeScript check passes. The in-app browser displayed `sort=votes` as «Народный рейтинг» and loaded the GTA RP cards. Live Turnstile remains **BLOCKED** until a real sitekey/secret and approved hostname are available. Local low-risk voting remains possible. No new resources or costs.

**Rollback:** the migration adds a column/index and does not rewrite accepted votes; disable client challenge rendering by leaving the paired keys unset, then roll back the application commit. Do not drop a used risk column before reading its configured threshold. The private ranking source stays PostgreSQL.

## Slice 4a: private Coins ledger and Support (local)

Migration `004_coins.sql` adds integer nonnegative balances, an append-only ledger, and per-server Support records in PostgreSQL. Support deducts Coins and writes its ledger entry and public nickname decision in one private transaction; a per-user lock and idempotency key guard concurrent and repeated requests. There is no transfer or withdrawal route. Purchases, Support, avatar, link slot, refund, and adjustment have distinct ledger kinds; only local `--local` mode exposes a clearly labeled test adjustment to exercise Support without a payment. One Coin has a displayed nominal value of 50 ₽; the unapproved discount grid and real sales are not enabled.

The private API returns aggregate Coins/participant counts and recent public names without exposing supporter user IDs. An opted-out or disallowed local nickname displays «Анонимно», while the transaction and ledger remain. The server page shows Support and its honest empty state for all three games; `/rating` and catalog `sort=support` use a separate Support order. People's rating still reads only accepted votes. OpenRouter review for ambiguous nicknames is pending. The Support aggregate is currently read from the private API; the eventual D1 public projection and lag monitoring remain necessary before a full cutover.

**Verified:** eight PGlite tests, including insufficient balance, 3-game Support, concurrent spending, idempotency, anonymity, ledger immutability, and no transfer endpoint. TypeScript and Vite build pass. The in-app browser showed the Support empty state on a GTA RP server, separate ranking toggle, and GTA RP catalog `sort=support` with equal cards. No paid resource created; 0 ₽ new cost.

**Rollback:** stop Support writes, revert this application commit, and preserve migration 004 data for reconciliation. Do not delete ledger rows or reverse transactions by editing them; a correction is a new ledger entry. This is a local-only database, with no production billing or migration.

## Slice 4b: Robokassa test adapter (local, provider credentials absent)

Migration `005_test_payments.sql` and the [test integration runbook](robokassa-test.md) add test-only orders, a signed POST checkout with `IsTest=1`, a merchant-configured `Receipt`, a server-to-server ResultURL, atomic one-time `PURCHASE` credit, and an admin reconciliation query. The ResultURL checks the test Password #2 signature, invoice, signed order nonce, exact amount and duplicate delivery before credit. A browser success redirect never credits Coins. Both checkout and ResultURL remain disabled until an explicit test switch and separate test credentials plus fiscal fields are configured. There is no live-sales mode or approved discounted package grid.

**Verified:** PGlite invariant tests cover invalid signatures/amounts, duplicate callbacks, atomic rollback on ledger failure, one-time credit, receipt sum, mandatory `IsTest=1`, and permission-gated reconciliation. TypeScript and Vite build pass. The browser-facing test purchase control appears only when test mode is configured; provider end-to-end remains **BLOCKED** on Robokassa test merchant credentials, test ResultURL access and merchant-approved fiscal values. No payment was sent to Robokassa, no production sales enabled, and no new paid resource created (0 ₽).

**Rollback:** disable the test switch, preserve orders and ledger for reconciliation, then revert the application commit. Migration 005 is additive and local only. Do not delete or edit ledger entries. A deployed migration needs a separate backup/rollback review.

## Slice 5a: private admin roles and read-only operations (local)

Migration `006_admin_roles.sql` defines ADMIN, MODERATOR, FINANCE, COPYRIGHT and SUPPORT roles with explicit section permissions. Effective rights are the union of role permissions and existing direct grants; owner access remains scoped by `gs_server_registry.owner_id`, so a player can also own a server. `/admin` shows only allowed sections: overview, servers, users, Claim review, votes, Support, Coins ledger, test payments and system events. Each private endpoint checks its own permission. Authorized admins can grant/revoke non-ADMIN roles with an audit entry; self-change and API assignment of ADMIN are blocked. The first verified, unbanned ADMIN can be provisioned once via `private-api/bootstrap-admin.mjs` with an operator-supplied reason after database deployment; that script has **not** been run.

**Verified:** 10 PGlite tests cover all section access denials, ADMIN access, role grant/revoke, self/ADMIN restrictions and audit. TypeScript check passes. The in-app browser showed `/admin` denying an unauthenticated viewer; an authenticated admin UI session has not been browser-tested yet. No new paid resources (0 ₽).

**Remaining in this stage:** server/user actions, moderation and copyright tabs, OpenRouter AI settings and review workflow. Production role bootstrap and live admin scenario depend on the private deployment. Rollback: revert application changes while retaining additive migration 006 and role/audit records; revoke an erroneous role with a recorded compensating action, not by editing audit history.

## Slice 5b: asynchronous nickname review (local)

Migration `007_nickname_review.sql` queues only ambiguous public nicknames after a Support transaction. The local filter allows ordinary names at zero cost, immediately anonymizes disallowed names, and leaves ambiguous names as «Анонимно» pending review. When `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` and an explicit `OPENROUTER_NICKNAME_MODEL` are configured on the private API, a leased worker sends only the nickname to OpenRouter and accepts `ALLOW / ANONYMOUS / REVIEW`. Failures retry with backoff; no AI request runs inside the Coins/Support transaction. A moderator can resolve pending or uncertain names with a reason and audit entry; a moderator cannot review their own support. Ledger and balance never change on AI failure or nickname decision.

**Verified:** 11 PGlite tests include provider failure and retry, manual moderation, permissions, unchanged Support ledger, and a mocked OpenRouter request without email. TypeScript check passes. [OpenRouter chat completion](https://openrouter.ai/docs/api/api-reference/chat/send-chat-completion-request) was checked; live model calls and pricing remain **BLOCKED** on a selected model, API key and an approved per-request budget. 0 ₽ new cost because no external call or resource was made.

**Rollback:** stop the nickname worker by removing its key/model, keep pending rows anonymous, and revert the code while retaining review/audit records. A completed decision currently needs an audited operator correction; the moderator re-review UI is not yet implemented. Do not alter financial records.

## Slice 6a: scoped copyright link workflow (local)

The public `/copyright` form, private migration `008_copyright.sql`, D1 migration `0008_public_restrictions.sql`, review/restore API and [workflow runbook](copyright-workflow.md) implement an exact-link complaint for site/Discord/Telegram slots. A complete claim is checked against the public card, queued for AI triage, and restricted only after a `VALID`/`DOUBTFUL` analysis or an audited human decision. `FAKE` does not disable a link; incomplete or mismatched scope requests clarification. A temporary restriction changes only the named slot, keeps the card, and has a manual RESTORE action. Private transaction + monotonic public outbox avoid a dual-database atomic write. The Worker strips restricted URLs from its public DTO after delivery; the private-mode profile fails closed on link loading. Claimant identity/evidence stay private. Telegram outbox contains only event, claim ID, server ID, scope and classification.

**Verified:** 12 PGlite tests cover complete/incomplete/fake triage, overlapping active claims, scoped restore, rollback and retry when the projection insert fails, permissions, no legal PII in the public snapshot or Telegram payload, and a mocked AI/Telegram request. Build passes. D1 migration 0008 was applied **locally only**. The actual local Worker HTTP response hid the `site` link for a version-1 test projection and restored it at version 2. The in-app browser showed the public form and required fields; provider delivery has not been tested. OpenRouter/Telegram credentials, a real email sender, and paid-model budget remain **BLOCKED**. 0 ₽ new cost.

## Slice 7a: crawlable build snapshots (local)

`scripts/public-pages.mjs` now derives route-specific visible HTML from the confirmed import dataset. `scripts/prerender-public.mjs` writes factual catalog/game/world/server pages plus home, owners and copyright, with route-specific title, description, canonical, generic WebPage JSON-LD and internal links. Unknown online is explicitly unknown; the snapshot contains no external server URLs, votes, paid positions or fake statistics. The build also writes `noindex` HTML for account/admin/dashboard and thin dynamic rating/openings pages. `scripts/sitemap.mjs` lists only 117 routes that get indexable prerendered HTML; the generated `public/sitemap.xml` is a build artifact. The existing React app replaces the visible snapshot after the API initializes.

**Verified:** `npm run build` and `SEO_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:4180 npm run test:seo` passed. Local Cloudflare Pages dev (not Vite Preview) returned the correct route-specific HTML over HTTP for `/`, `/catalog?game=gtarp`, `/lineage-2`, `/minecraft`, `/gta-rp`, `/server/new-day-rp`, `/copyright`, `/rating` and `/admin`; the latter two carried `noindex`. The in-app browser showed visible catalog cards without API data. Cloudflare Pages' clean-URL handling was necessary: Vite Preview serves `/catalog/` correctly but falls back to `/` for `/catalog`. [Cloudflare Pages local development](https://developers.cloudflare.com/pages/functions/local-development/) and [routing](https://developers.cloudflare.com/pages/functions/routing/) were checked. No cloud resource or cost was added (0 ₽).

An owner-added, removed or newly moderated card is not reflected until a rebuild; the Pages test did not supply the API, so browser hydration there showed a network error over the visible snapshot. Do not deploy/index server snapshots until they have been reconciled against the current published D1 projection or replaced with live D1-backed HTML. Unknown route fallback can still return the root shell with 200; a proper HTTP 404 gate is pending. Search Console, Яндекс Вебмастер, Bing and IndexNow remain **BLOCKED** on site access and verification credentials. Rollback is the previous build command without the post-build prerender step; no database state changed.

## Slice 6b: projection delay alert (local)

Private migration `009_projection_alerts.sql` records the current operational alert state. The backend checks its public outbox every 30 seconds: pending events older than `PUBLIC_PROJECTION_ALERT_SECONDS` (default 300, allowed 60–3600), or events with at least three delivery attempts, open a single `projection.lag` alert. Recovery closes it. Both transitions are audited and queued for Telegram with only counts and timing, never an event payload, user ID or secret. The admin system endpoint and tab show the alert state. Repeat checks do not flood Telegram. Delivery still uses the existing leased, retried Telegram outbox.

**Verified:** PGlite test covers threshold and retry triggers, deduplication, recovery, admin visibility and removal of private payload from messages. The restarted local private API applied migration 009 without a production change; TypeScript and build pass. Live Telegram delivery and public projection delivery are **BLOCKED** on the private deployment and credentials. No paid resource was created (0 ₽).

**Rollback:** stop the background service and revert the application commit while preserving alert, audit and Telegram outbox rows; migration 009 is additive. Do not drop the table during a live alert. A production rollout must apply the reviewed migration before running this service and include a backup/restore plan.

## Slice 6c: OpenRouter balance warning (local)

The private backend can check `GET /api/v1/credits` once an hour with a separately provided `OPENROUTER_MANAGEMENT_KEY`. This endpoint requires a management key according to [OpenRouter's credits documentation](https://openrouter.ai/docs/api/api-reference/credits/get-credits); the ordinary inference key is never assumed to have this permission. `OPENROUTER_LOW_BALANCE_USD` sets the warning threshold (default 5 USD, allowed 0.01–1000). A low balance, failure to check and recovery each use the private deduplicated alert state, audit and Telegram outbox. The alert text contains no key or AI prompt. With no management key, the monitor makes no request and reports no observed balance.

**Verified:** mocked PGlite test covers the official response shape, disabled mode, one low-balance warning during repeated checks, provider 403, recovery and omission of the key from messages. No real OpenRouter request or charge occurred (0 ₽). Live acceptance remains **BLOCKED** on a management key, an approved paid-model budget and Telegram credentials; approval is needed before any billable model calls. Rollback: remove the management key to stop checks, then revert this application change while retaining alert/audit rows.

## Slice 5c: owner summary edit with human review (local)

Migration `010_owner_summary.sql` gives a verified owner of a specific server a way to submit one pending factual summary at a time. The private `/account` page shows this form on owned L2, Minecraft and GTA RP cards. The admin's new “Правки серверов” tab requires `servers.review`; the reviewer cannot approve their own submission. Rejection leaves the public card untouched. Approval and the owner edit, server version, audit entry and public outbox snapshot commit in one PostgreSQL transaction. The public snapshot carries only the approved summary, Claim flag and scoped link restrictions. D1 updates `servers.summary` only when its monotonic projection version matches, in the same D1 batch as the version write; stale deliveries cannot replace a newer summary. No approval depends on an atomic write across databases.

**Verified:** targeted PGlite test exercises Claim, ownership isolation, self-review denial, duplicate pending edit, approval and rejection in all three games, and rollback when enqueue fails. SQLite executes the actual D1 projection SQL for newer/stale versions and a simulated failed update; TypeScript check passes. The local private API restarted with migration 010 and denied unauthenticated admin access over HTTP. Live private→D1 delivery is still **BLOCKED** on the deployment and projection secret. No new cloud resource or cost (0 ₽).

**Limitations:** this first owner operation covers a neutral short summary only. Version/rates/mode/opening date/region, media, external links, monitoring and vote integration still need separately scoped edits and moderation. The build-time SEO snapshot will not reflect an approved edit until the public HTML source is reconciled with D1. Rollback: stop new submissions, revert application code, preserve submitted decisions/audit/outbox, and roll forward with compensating approved text if a public correction is needed; never delete a delivered projection version.

## Slice 7c: public HTML/catalog release reconciliation (local)

`npm run seo:reconcile -- https://PUBLIC_API_ORIGIN/` is a read-only release gate after `npm run build`. ## Slice 5f: audited user moderation (local)

Private migration `013_user_moderation.sql` gives only ADMIN the `users.manage` permission. The admin user list can block or unblock a non-admin account with a required reason. A block locks the user row, records `banned_at`, removes active sessions and writes an audit event in one PostgreSQL transaction. Existing votes, Claims, Support, Coins, payments and ledger stay intact. Repeated action is a no-op without duplicate audit; unblocking never revives a revoked session. Self-blocking and blocking another ADMIN through this route are denied.

**Verified:** targeted moderation test and the full 21/21 private-core suite passed, including session invalidation, role boundaries, one audit event per transition, retained ledger and fresh login after unblocking. TypeScript check passed; local private API restarted with migration 013. Authenticated admin browser interaction and deployed Yandex acceptance remain open. No new cloud resource or cost (0 ₽).

**Rollback:** disable the UI/API route or revert code while retaining `banned_at` and audit history. A mistaken block is reversed by an audited UNBAN, followed by a new login; never restore a revoked session or erase the original event. Migration 013 is additive and has not run in production.

## Slice 0e/2h: importer respects private Claim projection (local)

The curated D1 importer previously protected only legacy `servers.owner_id`. A new Claim is authoritative in Yandex and projects just `public_server_projection.claimed` to D1, so a repeat import could rewrite a claimed card's reviewed facts and monitoring source. Its SQL now requires **both** no legacy owner and no projected Claim for fact updates, target/source inserts or updates, source disabling and archive-missing. The import planner omits claimed cards from its archive list. This preserves the private/public boundary: no owner ID or Claim evidence is copied into D1, and public import correctness requires no atomic write across stores.

**Verified locally:** the eight dataset tests passed. An isolated D1 acceptance imported 96 confirmed records, repeated the import without duplicates, changed an unclaimed fact, and verified that legacy and projected Claims protected facts, monitoring target and online sources. Archive-missing left both kinds of claimed card published while archiving an unclaimed card. It opened every imported public profile and confirmed unknown L2 player counts; the completed report is in ignored `output/import-*/result.json`. The main local three-game catalog was not modified. New resource cost 0 ₽. Production importer remains untouched until the private Claim projection and migration are deployed in the approved order. Rollback: stop imports; if an earlier import already overwrote an approved public value, replay the latest private snapshot at a higher version after review. Do not alter the private Claim to satisfy the importer.

## Slice 5h: Claim-scoped public monitor target (local)

Private migration `018_owner_monitoring.sql` gives an email-confirmed owner a server-scoped target setting in `/account`. MC accepts Server List Ping host/port, L2 accepts login-server host/port without a player count, and GTA RP accepts a bare FiveM origin. Private/reserved addresses, URL secrets/fragments, relays, unsupported methods and AGENT tokens are rejected. The private transaction stores the setting and audit, increments one version and queues a public, token-free snapshot; an identical setting is a no-op. D1 applies only newer versions and clears a prior count to UNKNOWN while awaiting a fresh probe; disabling the target also hides stale online. The reviewed Claim projection protects the target from later dataset imports. [Rollout and rollback](owner-monitoring.md) are documented.

**Verified locally:** the focused PGlite/SQLite test covers owner isolation, valid targets in all three games, invalid destinations, idempotency, PII-free snapshot, D1 target/online atomic batch, a failed target write rolling back the public version, disabling and stale-version replay. The existing isolated monitor acceptance passed in the preceding slice. The actual local Worker/private HTTP handoff for this new setting and a real published-server probe remain unverified; Yandex deployment is still BLOCKED on access/resources. No production migration or new paid resource; new cost 0 ₽. Rollback uses a newer disable/corrected target and retains audit/history.

## Slice 0m: private PostgreSQL and API running on staging VM

The owner added a named OS Login SSH public key; a source-only archive was transferred by SCP after checking the VM host fingerprint and SHA-256. The host listens on TCP 22 only; `5432`, `8080`, `80` and `443` are not published. Reconcile prerender snapshots with live D1, validate the Yandex static-routing candidate over staging HTTP, connect webmaster services when access exists, and complete authenticated browser/deployed end-to-end tests, mobile/error acceptance, rollback runbook and release review. The existing ADMIN session also ran the read-only ledger reconciliation from the Payments tab. The UI returned zero order mismatches and zero balance mismatches, with no payment records. This verifies the live reconciliation endpoint on an empty production ledger; it does not verify Robokassa notification delivery, crediting, refund or reconciliation with real transactions.

Read-only D1 row counts found zero legacy users, sessions, votes, Claims, identities, auth codes, boost orders, logos, active vote API keys, Votifier tokens, webhook URLs and legacy claimed servers. There are 15 inactive vote-integration rows and **32 historical `server_events` rows with visitor hashes** (28 views, three play clicks, one Claim start, 22–26.09.2026). `PUBLIC_ONLY` now refuses the event write route, so the sampled legacy write path is closed. The historical hashes remain in Cloudflare and need a reviewed archive/aggregation and deletion plan; deleting or anonymizing them is an irreversible data change and was not done. This is a concrete remaining gap in the private/public boundary.

## 29.09.2026 signed projection acceptance (local)

An isolated Wrangler/D1 runtime test now applies the public migrations, seeds one disposable card and runs the Worker with `PUBLIC_ONLY=true`. It verifies private route refusal; an invalid projection secret returns 403; an unexpected private email field returns 400; a valid signed snapshot updates the public summary and Support counters; duplicate and older versions leave that result intact; and a newer version replaces it. The test passed with no production D1 writes or new cloud cost. This establishes the local receiver behavior, **not** end-to-end delivery from the deployed Yandex outbox: production `public_server_projection` is still empty and the private admin queue had no pending item during the read-only check. Next acceptance needs a controlled real private mutation on a test-owned server and observation of D1 version, lag and retry; do not fabricate a Claim/vote for a real third-party server.

The test exposed a same-version conflict: the monotonic projection row was unchanged, but follow-on card fields could still be overwritten by a different payload at that version. Additive D1 migration `0011_projection_attempt_token.sql` records a per-delivery token only when the version advances; every follow-on field update checks that token inside the same D1 batch. A conflicting same-version replay is now a complete no-op, including summary and Support aggregate. The local Worker test, all 32 private-core tests and TypeScript/Worker typecheck passed. The rollback for deployment is to restore the previous Worker version; the extra column can remain unused and no data is deleted. Apply the D1 migration **before** deploying the new Worker code.

**Production deployment blocked:** read-only Wrangler inspection confirmed `0011_projection_attempt_token.sql` is the only pending remote migration. The automatic approval review rejected the attempted D1 apply as a production schema change requiring specific user approval under the existing migration restriction. The command did not run. Do not deploy this Worker revision before that migration; the deployed prior revision remains active. After approval, recheck the D1 migration plan, apply only 0011, deploy the existing three Workers, verify the public routes and signed delivery, and retain their current version IDs above for rollback. No production schema or Worker changed in this second slice.

## 30.09.2026 one account, server-scoped owner status (local, deployment pending)

The private API continues to use one user account. `OWNER` is derived from at least one private `gs_server_registry.owner_id` grant; there is no global owner permission over unrelated servers. Claim decisions now require the `ADMIN` role specifically, in addition to `claims.review`, so a moderator/support permission alone cannot confirm ownership. The admin Users list shows each account's player/owner status and pending Claim count. Its per-user “Изменить статус ЛК” panel can confirm or reject that user's specific pending Claim with a written reason, or revoke that user's existing grant on a specific server. Revocation clears a former owner's monitoring override, increments the private server version, enqueues a public snapshot and appends an audit record in one PostgreSQL transaction. A repeat revoke does not enqueue again. Historical verified Claims remain in the append-only decision history; `/owners/me/claims` now reports whether that grant is still active. A new Claim and fresh admin approval can restore rights. The user's Account page labels the status and exposes owner tools only for servers returned by the private ownership query.

**Verified locally:** 33/33 private-core tests, including non-admin denial, two-server isolation, revocation rollback on outbox failure, loss of the last OWNER status and renewed Claim; frontend typecheck and build; in-app browser on local `/account` and `/admin` showed the new status and per-user control with an existing disposable admin/player. The local browser panel displayed the player's one claimed DEMO GTA RP server and a disabled revoke button until a reason is entered. No production account or ownership was changed. No schema migration or new resource/cost. Rollback is to redeploy the previous private API/UI image or revert this commit; already committed admin decisions would need a new reviewed Claim/revocation, never a blind DB restore. **Still open:** deploy the private API and Pages UI together, verify live ADMIN and player sessions, and decide when to reopen currently closed registration; do not claim live onboarding works yet.

## 30.09.2026 initial public projection replay (prepared locally)

The private registry has imported cards, but the live D1 `public_server_projection` was empty at the last read-only check and the outbox had no pending rows. Added `private-api/backfill-public-projection.mjs`: its default mode fetches the published L2/Minecraft/GTA RP cards, reconciles every public ID, game and slug against the private registry, and reports how many cards remain at version zero without writing. `--apply` locks the matching private rows, advances only version-zero records to version one and enqueues their allowlisted public snapshots in the same PostgreSQL transaction. The ordinary minute maintenance job then delivers the signed outbox; replay after success does not enqueue duplicates. It never changes catalog names, base facts, ownership or financial records. A count/identity mismatch aborts before writing, and an outbox failure rolls back all versions.

The three focused PGlite tests cover the default read-only plan, one initial snapshot per game, repeated apply, mismatch and transaction rollback; the full private suite passed 36/36. Production `--apply` is **not run** because the private database still lacks a verified current off-VM restore. Deploy and run the read-only mode first, confirm exact counts and snapshot policy, complete backup/restore, then apply and compare the resulting D1 versions and lag; do not treat a zero pending queue alone as delivery proof. No cloud resource or fixed cost was added. Rollback before apply is code-only; after apply, do not decrease versions or delete outbox rows—issue a newer compensating snapshot if a public field needs correction.

**Live read-only check:** the script was transferred to the existing VM with an exact SHA-256 match, copied temporarily into the running API container and executed without `--apply`. It returned `{mode:"dry-run",registry:70,initial:70,enqueued:0}`: all 70 private cards match the published core IDs/game/slugs and all still need an initial version-one snapshot. The temporary copies were removed afterward. No version or outbox row was written. This removes the catalog-identity uncertainty; the backup/restore gate and subsequent signed D1 acceptance remain open.
